      <div class="section" id="stat.171.306">
        <h1 class="shn">171.306 IGNITION INTERLOCK DEVICE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.306.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) As used in this section, the terms in this subdivision have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Ignition interlock device" or "device" means equipment that is designed to measure breath alcohol concentration and to prevent a motor vehicle's ignition from being started by a person whose breath alcohol concentration measures 0.02 or higher on the equipment.</p>
          <p>(c) "Location tracking capabilities" means the ability of an electronic or wireless device to identify and transmit its geographic location through the operation of the device.</p>
          <p>(d) "Program participant" means a person who has qualified to take part in the ignition interlock program under this section, and whose driver's license has been:</p>
          <p>(1) revoked, canceled, or denied under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.52">169A.52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.54">169A.54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.04#stat.171.04.1">171.04, subdivision 1</a>, clause (10); or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.177">171.177</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) revoked under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.17#stat.171.17.1">171.17, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a), clause (1), or suspended under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.187">171.187</a>, for a violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.2113#stat.609.2113.1">609.2113, subdivision 1</a>, clause (2), item (i) or (iii), (3), or (4); subdivision 2, clause (2), item (i) or (iii), (3), or (4); or subdivision 3, clause (2), item (i) or (iii), (3), or (4); or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.2114#stat.609.2114.2">609.2114, subdivision 2</a>, clause (2), item (i) or (iii), (3), or (4), resulting in bodily harm, substantial bodily harm, or great bodily harm.</p>
          <p>(e) "Qualified prior impaired driving incident" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.03#stat.169A.03.22">169A.03, subdivision 22</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.306.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Performance standards; certification; manufacturer and provider requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall establish performance standards and a process for certifying devices used in the ignition interlock program, except that the commissioner may not establish standards that, directly or indirectly, require devices to use or enable location tracking capabilities without a court order.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The manufacturer of a device must apply annually for certification of the device by submitting the form prescribed by the commissioner. The commissioner shall require manufacturers of certified devices to:</p>
          <p>(1) provide device installation, servicing, and monitoring to indigent program participants at a discounted rate, according to the standards established by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(2) include in an ignition interlock device contract a provision that a program participant who voluntarily terminates participation in the program is only liable for servicing and monitoring costs incurred during the time the device is installed on the motor vehicle, regardless of whether the term of the contract has expired; and</p>
          <p>(3) include in an ignition interlock device contract a provision that requires manufacturers of certified devices to pay any towing or repair costs caused by device failure or malfunction, or by damage caused during device installation, servicing, or monitoring.</p>
          <p>(c) The manufacturer of a certified device must include with an ignition interlock device contract a separate notice to the program participant regarding any location tracking capabilities of the device.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.306.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Program requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall establish guidelines for participation in the ignition interlock program. A person who seeks to participate in the program shall sign a written acknowledgment that the person has received, reviewed, and agreed to abide by the program guidelines.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commissioner must enter a notation on a person's driving record to indicate that the person is a program participant.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A person under the age of 18 years is not eligible to be a program participant.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A program participant shall pay costs associated with an ignition interlock device on every motor vehicle that the participant operates or intends to operate.</p>
          <p>(e) A program participant shall participate in any treatment recommended in a chemical use assessment report.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) A program participant shall bring the device-equipped motor vehicle or vehicles operated by the program participant to an approved service provider for device calibration and servicing according to the schedule established by the commissioner and as indicated by the ignition interlock device.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) The commissioner shall not permit location tracking capabilities on any ignition interlock device to be enabled except as provided in this paragraph. The commissioner shall require the activation of location tracking capabilities on an ignition interlock device when ordered by a court.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.306.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Off-road recreational vehicles and motorboats.</span></h2>
          <p>A program participant in the ignition interlock device program may operate an off-road recreational vehicle or a motorboat only if it is equipped with an approved ignition interlock device as provided under this section and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/84.765#stat.84.765.2">84.765, subdivision 2</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/86B.33#stat.86B.33.2">86B.33, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.306.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Issuance of restricted license.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall issue a class D driver's license, subject to the applicable limitations and restrictions of this section, to a program participant who meets the requirements of this section and the program guidelines. The commissioner shall not issue a license unless the program participant has provided satisfactory proof that:</p>
          <p>(1) a certified ignition interlock device has been installed on the participant's motor vehicle at an installation service center designated by the device's manufacturer; and</p>
          <p>(2) the participant has insurance coverage on the vehicle equipped with the ignition interlock device. If the participant has previously been convicted of violating section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.791">169.791</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.793">169.793</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.797">169.797</a> or the participant's license has previously been suspended, revoked, or canceled under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.792">169.792</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.797">169.797</a>, the commissioner shall require the participant to present an insurance identification card that is certified by the insurance company to be noncancelable for a period not to exceed 12 months.</p>
          <p>(b) A license issued under authority of this section must contain a restriction prohibiting the program participant from driving, operating, or being in physical control of any motor vehicle not equipped with a functioning ignition interlock device certified by the commissioner. A participant may drive an employer-owned vehicle not equipped with an interlock device while in the normal course and scope of employment duties pursuant to the program guidelines established by the commissioner and with the employer's written consent.</p>
          <p>(c) A program participant whose driver's license has been: (1) revoked under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.52#stat.169A.52.3">169A.52, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (a), clause (1), (2), or (3), or subdivision 4, paragraph (a), clause (1), (2), or (3); <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.54#stat.169A.54.1">169A.54, subdivision 1</a>, clause (1), (2), (3), or (4); or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.177">171.177</a>, subdivision 4, paragraph (a), clause (1), (2), or (3), or subdivision 5, paragraph (a), clause (1), (2), or (3); or (2) revoked under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.17#stat.171.17.1">171.17, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a), clause (1), or suspended under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.187">171.187</a>, for a violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.2113#stat.609.2113.1">609.2113, subdivision 1</a>, clause (2), item (i) or (iii), (3), or (4); subdivision 2, clause (2), item (i) or (iii), (3), or (4); or subdivision 3, clause (2), item (i) or (iii), (3), or (4); or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.2114#stat.609.2114.2">609.2114, subdivision 2</a>, clause (2), item (i) or (iii), (3), or (4), resulting in bodily harm, substantial bodily harm, or great bodily harm, where the participant has fewer than two qualified prior impaired driving incidents within the past ten years or fewer than three qualified prior impaired driving incidents ever; may apply for conditional reinstatement of the driver's license, subject to the ignition interlock restriction.</p>
          <p>(d) A program participant whose driver's license has been: (1) revoked, canceled, or denied under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.52#stat.169A.52.3">169A.52, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (a), clause (4), (5), or (6), or subdivision 4, paragraph (a), clause (4), (5), or (6); <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.54#stat.169A.54.1">169A.54, subdivision 1</a>, clause (5), (6), or (7); or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.177">171.177</a>, subdivision 4, paragraph (a), clause (4), (5), or (6), or subdivision 5, paragraph (a), clause (4), (5), or (6); or (2) revoked under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.17#stat.171.17.1">171.17, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a), clause (1), or suspended under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.187">171.187</a>, for a violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.2113">609.2113</a>, subdivision 1, clause (2), item (i) or (iii), (3), or (4); subdivision 2, clause (2), item (i) or (iii), (3), or (4); or subdivision 3, clause (2), item (i) or (iii), (3), or (4); or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.2114#stat.609.2114.2">609.2114, subdivision 2</a>, clause (2), item (i) or (iii), (3), or (4), resulting in bodily harm, substantial bodily harm, or great bodily harm, where the participant has two or more qualified prior impaired driving incidents within the past ten years or three or more qualified prior impaired driving incidents ever; may apply for conditional reinstatement of the driver's license, subject to the ignition interlock restriction, if the program participant is enrolled in a licensed substance use disorder treatment or rehabilitation program as recommended in a chemical use assessment. As a prerequisite to eligibility for eventual reinstatement of full driving privileges, a participant whose chemical use assessment recommended treatment or rehabilitation shall complete a licensed substance use disorder treatment or rehabilitation program. If the program participant's ignition interlock device subsequently registers a positive breath alcohol concentration of 0.02 or higher, the commissioner shall extend the time period that the participant must participate in the program until the participant has reached the required abstinence period described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.55#stat.169A.55.4">169A.55, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) Notwithstanding any statute or rule to the contrary, the commissioner has authority to determine when a program participant is eligible for restoration of full driving privileges, except that the commissioner shall not reinstate full driving privileges until the program participant has met all applicable prerequisites for reinstatement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.55">169A.55</a> and until the program participant's device has registered no positive breath alcohol concentrations of 0.02 or higher during the preceding 90 days.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.306.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Penalties; program violations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a program participant tampers with, circumvents, or bypasses a device; drives, operates, or exercises physical control over a motor vehicle not equipped with a device certified by the commissioner; violates a condition of a license conditionally reinstated under subdivision 4 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.30">171.30</a>; or violates the program guidelines of subdivision 3, the commissioner shall extend the person's revocation period under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.52">169A.52</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.54">169A.54</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.177">171.177</a> by:</p>
          <p>(1) 180 days for a first violation;</p>
          <p>(2) one year for a second violation; or</p>
          <p>(3) 545 days for a third and each subsequent violation.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), the commissioner may terminate participation in the program by any person when, in the commissioner's judgment, termination is necessary to the interests of public safety and welfare. In the event of termination, the commissioner shall not reduce the applicable revocation period under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.52">169A.52</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.54">169A.54</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.177">171.177 </a>by the amount of time during which the person possessed a limited or restricted driver's license issued under the authority of subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.306.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Penalties; tampering.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person who lends, rents, or leases a motor vehicle that is not equipped with a functioning ignition interlock device certified by the commissioner to a person with a license issued under this section knowing that the person is subject to the ignition interlock restriction is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
          <p>(b) A person who tampers with, circumvents, or bypasses the ignition interlock device, or assists another to tamper with, circumvent, or bypass the device, is guilty of a misdemeanor except when the action was taken for emergency purposes or for mechanical repair, and the person limited to the use of an ignition interlock device does not operate the motor vehicle while the device is disengaged.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.306.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Venue.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to the provisions of <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/court_rules/cr/id/24/">Rule 24</a> of the Rules of Criminal Procedure and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/627.01">627.01</a>, a violation of subdivision 6 or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.09#stat.171.09.1">171.09, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (g), may be prosecuted in:</p>
          <p>(1) the county in which the vehicle involved in the offense is found;</p>
          <p>(2) the county in which the accused resides;</p>
          <p>(3) any county through which the vehicle traveled in the course of the trip during or after which the offense was committed; or</p>
          <p>(4) the county in which the impaired driving incident occurred, which resulted in the accused being issued a driver's license with an ignition interlock restriction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.306.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Rulemaking.</span></h2>
          <p>In establishing the performance standards and certification process of subdivision 2, the program guidelines of subdivision 3, and any other rules necessary to implement this section, the commissioner is subject to chapter 14.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.306.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Choice of vendor.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A judicial officer, county agency, or probation office may not require or suggest that a person participating in the ignition interlock program under this section use a particular ignition interlock vendor but may provide the person with a list of all Minnesota vendors of certified devices.</p>
          <p>(b) Paragraph (a) does not apply in counties where a contract exists for a specific vendor to provide interlock device service for program participants who are indigent pursuant to subdivision 2, paragraph (b), clause (1).</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.306.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/54/#laws.3.5.0">2007 c 54 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/29/#laws.0.2.0">2009 c 29 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/366/#laws.0.14.0">2010 c 366 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/187/#laws.1.26.0">2012 c 187 art 1 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/287/#laws.4.37.0">2012 c 287 art 4 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/298/#laws.0.1.0">2012 c 298 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/117/#laws.3.17.0">2013 c 117 art 3 s 17</a>,18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/180/#laws.0.9.0">2014 c 180 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/298/#laws.0.1.0">2014 c 298 s 1</a>,5,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/83/#laws.1.1.0">2017 c 83 art 1 s 1</a>-4; art 3 s 18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/6/#laws.3.11.0">1Sp2021 c 6 art 3 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/11/#laws.2.9.0">1Sp2021 c 11 art 2 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/55/#laws.1.99.0">2022 c 55 art 1 s 99</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.51.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/52/#laws.5.8.0">2023 c 52 art 5 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/68/#laws.5.39.0">2023 c 68 art 5 s 39</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.31">
        <h1 class="shn">171.31 INFORMATION ON PERSON RECEIVING BLINDNESS BENEFITS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of public safety, in order to promote highway safety by restricting driving privileges to those persons meeting accepted visual acuity standards, may request and shall receive information concerning the identity and whereabouts of any person who has applied for or received any type of welfare, licensing, or other benefits for the blind or nearly blind, from the records of all departments, boards, bureaus, or other agencies of this state except the Department of Revenue, and they shall provide such information notwithstanding the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/268.19">268.19</a> or any other existing law or rule to the contrary, except that section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270B.02#stat.270B.02.1">270B.02, subdivision 1</a>, prohibits disclosure of information by the commissioner of revenue.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.31.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/524/#laws.0.1.0">1967 c 524 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.1.18.0">1969 c 1129 art 1 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/514/#laws.3.1.0">1984 c 514 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/184/#laws.2.7.0">1989 c 184 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/66/#laws.0.79.0">1997 c 66 s 79</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.32">
        <h1 class="shn">171.32 ACTION UPON INFORMATION RELATING TO BLINDNESS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.32.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Actions by commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner upon receipt of any such information shall take such action as the commissioner deems necessary to insure that each such person meets the accepted visual acuity standards required of all driver's license applicants and such further action as required by law or rule. The driver's license of any person who has been classified as legally blind shall be immediately canceled.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.32.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Visual acuity examination.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person who is notified that the person's driver's license may be canceled because of a defect of visual acuity pursuant to the provisions of this section may demand and shall receive the standard visual acuity examination required of all driver's license applicants, or may be examined by a qualified physician of the person's choice.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.32.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Use of examination data.</span></h2>
          <p>The results of any visual acuity examination administered because of information received pursuant to this section may be communicated by the commissioner to the department from which the person received a benefit. The information received by the commissioner pursuant to this section must not be divulged or otherwise made known in any manner except in connection with the cancellation of drivers' licenses, and then only to the person involved whose license is canceled, and except for statistical purposes which do not reveal the identity of the individuals involved. The record of such person with respect to visual acuity shall be maintained in the same manner as all other driver's license records.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.32.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/524/#laws.0.2.0">1967 c 524 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.321">
        <h1 class="shn">171.321 QUALIFICATIONS OF SCHOOL BUS AND TYPE III VEHICLE DRIVERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.321.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Endorsement.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">No person shall drive a school bus when transporting school children to or from school or upon a school-related trip or activity without having a valid class A, class B, or class C driver's license with a school bus endorsement except that a person possessing a valid driver's license but not a school bus endorsement may drive a type III vehicle or a school bus, subject to the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.02">171.02</a>, subdivisions 2, 2a, and 2b.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.321.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner of public safety shall prescribe rules governing (1) the physical qualifications of school bus drivers and tests required to obtain a school bus endorsement, and (2) the physical qualifications of type III vehicle drivers.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The rules under paragraph (a) must provide that an applicant for a school bus endorsement or renewal is exempt from the physical qualifications and medical examination required to operate a school bus upon providing evidence of being medically examined and certified within the preceding 24 months as physically qualified to operate a commercial motor vehicle, pursuant to Code of Federal Regulations, title 49, part 391, subpart E, or rules of the commissioner of transportation incorporating those federal regulations. The commissioner shall accept physical examinations for school bus drivers conducted by medical examiners authorized as provided by Code of Federal Regulations, title 49, chapter 3, part 391, subpart E.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The commissioner of public safety, in conjunction with the commissioner of education, shall adopt rules prescribing a training program for Head Start bus drivers. The program must provide for initial classroom and behind-the-wheel training, and annual in-service training. The program must provide training in defensive driving, human relations, emergency and accident procedures, vehicle maintenance, traffic laws, and use of safety equipment. The program must provide that the training will be conducted by the contract operator for a Head Start agency, the Head Start grantee, a licensed driver training school, or by another person or entity approved by both commissioners.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The commissioner may exempt a type III vehicle driver from the physical qualifications required to operate a type III vehicle upon receiving evidence of the driver having been medically examined and certified within the preceding 24 months as physically qualified to operate a commercial motor vehicle as provided for applicants for a school bus endorsement under paragraph (b).</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The actions of the commissioner of public safety in establishing physical qualifications for type III vehicle drivers are not rulemaking for purposes of chapter 14, are not subject to the Administrative Procedure Act contained in chapter 14, and are not subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.321.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Records check of applicant.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Before issuing or renewing a school bus endorsement, the commissioner shall conduct a criminal history and driver's license records check of the applicant. The commissioner may also conduct the check at any time while a person is so licensed. The check must consist of a criminal history check of the state criminal records repository and a check of the driver's license records system. If the applicant has resided in Minnesota for less than five years, the check must also include a national criminal history check. The commissioner shall accept the national criminal history check request and the fingerprints of the applicant and is authorized to exchange fingerprints with the Federal Bureau of Investigation and request the Federal Bureau of Investigation to conduct a criminal history check. The applicant's failure to cooperate with the commissioner in conducting the records check is reasonable cause to deny an application or cancel a school bus endorsement. The commissioner may not release the results of the records check to any person except the applicant or the applicant's designee in writing.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may issue to an otherwise qualified applicant a temporary school bus endorsement, effective for no more than 180 days, upon presentation of (1) an affidavit by the applicant that the applicant has not been convicted of a disqualifying offense and (2) a criminal history check from each state of residence for the previous five years. The criminal history check may be conducted and prepared by any public or private source acceptable to the commissioner. The commissioner may reissue the temporary endorsement if the national criminal records repository check is timely submitted but not completed within the 180-day period.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.321.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Training.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) No person shall drive a class A, B, C, or D school bus when transporting school children to or from school or upon a school-related trip or activity without having demonstrated sufficient skills and knowledge to transport students in a safe and legal manner.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A bus driver must have training or experience that allows the driver to meet at least the following competencies:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) safely operate the type of school bus the driver will be driving;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) understand student behavior, including issues relating to students with disabilities;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) encourage orderly conduct of students on the bus and handle incidents of misconduct appropriately;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) know and understand relevant laws, rules of the road, and local school bus safety policies;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) handle emergency situations; and</p>
          <p class="in">(6) safely load and unload students.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The commissioner of public safety shall develop a comprehensive model school bus driver training program and model assessments for school bus driver training competencies, which are not subject to chapter 14. A school district, nonpublic school, or private contractor may use alternative assessments for bus driver training competencies with the approval of the commissioner of public safety. A driver may receive at least eight hours of school bus in-service training any year, as an alternative to being assessed for bus driver competencies after the initial year of being assessed for bus driver competencies. The employer shall keep the assessment or a record of the in-service training for the current period available for inspection by representatives of the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(d) A school district, nonpublic school, or private contractor shall provide in-service training annually to each school bus driver.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.321.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Annual evaluation and license verification.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, "annually" means at least once every 380 days from the initial or previous evaluation and at least once every 380 days from the initial or previous license verification.</p>
          <p>(b) A school district, nonpublic school, or private contractor shall annually verify with the National Driver Register or with the Department of Public Safety the validity of the driver's license of each employee who regularly transports students for the district in:</p>
          <p>(1) a type A school bus, a type B school bus, a type C school bus, or type D school bus;</p>
          <p>(2) a multifunction school activity bus; or</p>
          <p>(3) a type III vehicle.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.321.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>

<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.1.48.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 1 s 48</a>; art 10 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.29.0">2004 c 206 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/294/#laws.2.31.0">2004 c 294 art 2 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.8.13.0">2007 c 146 art 8 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/350/#laws.1.64.0">2008 c 350 art 1 s 64</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.8.12.0">2009 c 96 art 8 s 12</a>-14;
