      <div class="section" id="stat.171.3213">
        <h1 class="shn">171.3213 THIRD-PARTY TESTING OF SCHOOL BUS DRIVERS.</h1>
        <p>A school district that is a third-party testing program and owns or operates school buses may enter into an agreement with other school districts to test the other districts' school bus driver employees. A school bus company that is a third-party testing program and owns or operates school buses may enter into an agreement with other school bus companies to test the other companies' school bus driver employees. A third-party testing program may be reimbursed by the tested driver's school district or company. The agreement must be submitted to the commissioner for approval. A certified third-party tester must be employed by a school district or a school bus company providing the testing services.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.3213.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/3/#laws.3.77.0">1Sp2019 c 3 art 3 s 77</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.3215">
        <h1 class="shn">171.3215 CANCELING BUS ENDORSEMENT FOR CERTAIN OFFENSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.3215.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) As used in this section, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "School bus driver" means a person possessing a school bus driver's endorsement on a valid Minnesota driver's license or a person possessing a valid Minnesota driver's license who drives a vehicle with a seating capacity of ten or less persons used as a school bus.</p>
          <p>(c) "Disqualifying offense" includes (1) any felony offense, (2) any misdemeanor, gross misdemeanor, or felony violation of chapter 152, (3) any violation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.3451">609.3451</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.746#stat.609.746.1">609.746, subdivision 1</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/617.23">617.23</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/617.246">617.246</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/617.247">617.247</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/617.293">617.293</a>, or (4) while driving, operating, or being in physical control of a school bus or a Head Start bus, a violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.20">169A.20</a> or a similar statute or ordinance from another state.</p>
          <p>(d) "Head Start bus driver" means a person possessing a valid Minnesota driver's license:</p>
          <p>(1) with a passenger endorsement, who drives a Head Start bus;</p>
          <p>(2) with a school bus driver's endorsement, who drives a Head Start bus; or</p>
          <p>(3) who drives a vehicle with a seating capacity of ten or fewer persons used as a Head Start bus.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.3215.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Cancellation for disqualifying and other offenses.</span></h2>
          <p>Within ten days of receiving notice under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/631.40#stat.631.40.1a">631.40, subdivision 1a</a>, or otherwise receiving notice for a nonresident driver, that a school bus driver has been convicted of a disqualifying offense, the commissioner shall permanently cancel the school bus driver's endorsement on the offender's driver's license and in the case of a nonresident, the driver's privilege to operate a school bus in Minnesota. A school bus driver whose endorsement or privilege to operate a school bus in Minnesota has been permanently canceled may not apply for reinstatement. Within ten days of receiving notice under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/631.40#stat.631.40.1a">631.40, subdivision 1a</a>, or otherwise receiving notice for a nonresident driver, that a school bus driver has been convicted of a violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.20">169A.20</a>, or a similar statute or ordinance from another state, and within ten days of revoking a school bus driver's license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.52">169A.52</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.177">171.177</a>, the commissioner shall cancel the school bus driver's endorsement on the offender's driver's license or the nonresident's privilege to operate a school bus in Minnesota for five years. After five years, a school bus driver may apply to the commissioner for reinstatement. Even after five years, cancellation of a school bus driver's endorsement or a nonresident's privilege to operate a school bus in Minnesota for a violation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.20">169A.20</a>, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.50">169A.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.53">169A.53</a>, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.177">171.177</a>, or a similar statute or ordinance from another state, shall remain in effect until the driver provides proof of successful completion of an alcohol or controlled substance treatment program. For a first offense, proof of completion is required only if treatment was ordered as part of a chemical use assessment. Within ten days of receiving notice under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/631.40#stat.631.40.1a">631.40, subdivision 1a</a>, or otherwise receiving notice for a nonresident driver, that a school bus driver has been convicted of a fourth moving violation in the last three years, the commissioner shall cancel the school bus driver's endorsement on the offender's driver's license or the nonresident's privilege to operate a school bus in Minnesota until one year has elapsed since the last conviction. A school bus driver who has no new convictions after one year may apply for reinstatement. Upon canceling the offender's school bus driver's endorsement, the commissioner shall immediately notify the licensed offender of the cancellation in writing, by depositing in the United States post office a notice addressed to the licensed offender at the licensed offender's last known address, with postage prepaid thereon.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.3215.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Cancellation for crime against minor.</span></h2>
          <p>Within ten days of receiving notice that a Head Start bus driver has committed a crime against a minor, the commissioner shall permanently cancel the passenger endorsement on the offender's driver's license. Upon canceling the offender's passenger endorsement, the commissioner shall immediately notify the licensed offender of the cancellation in writing, by depositing in the United States post office a notice addressed to the licensed offender at the licensed offender's last known address, with postage prepaid thereon. For purposes of this subdivision, "crime against a minor" means an act committed against a minor victim that constitutes a violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.185">609.185</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.19">609.19</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.195">609.195</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.20">609.20</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.205">609.205</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.2112#stat.609.2112.1">609.2112, subdivision 1</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.2114#stat.609.2114.1">609.2114, subdivision 1</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.221">609.221</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.222">609.222</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.223">609.223</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.342">609.342</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.343">609.343</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.344">609.344</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.345">609.345</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.352">609.352</a> or Minnesota Statutes 2012, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.21#stat.609.21.1a">609.21, subdivision 1a</a>, paragraph (a), or a felony violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.322">609.322</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.324">609.324</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.377">609.377</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.3215.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Background check.</span></h2>
          <p>Before issuing or renewing a driver's license with a school bus driver's endorsement, the commissioner shall conduct an investigation to determine if the applicant has been convicted of committing a disqualifying offense, four moving violations in the previous three years, a violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.20">169A.20</a> or a similar statute or ordinance from another state, a gross misdemeanor, or if the applicant's driver's license has been revoked under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.52">169A.52</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.177">171.177</a>. The commissioner shall not issue a new bus driver's endorsement and shall not renew an existing bus driver's endorsement if the applicant has been convicted of committing a disqualifying offense. The commissioner shall not issue a new bus driver's endorsement and shall not renew an existing bus driver's endorsement if, within the previous five years, the applicant has been convicted of committing a violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.20">169A.20</a>, or a similar statute or ordinance from another state, a gross misdemeanor, or if the applicant's driver's license has been revoked under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.52">169A.52</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.177">171.177</a>, or if, within the previous three years, the applicant has been convicted of four moving violations. An applicant who has been convicted of violating section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.20">169A.20</a>, or a similar statute or ordinance from another state, or who has had a license revocation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.52">169A.52</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.177">171.177</a> within the previous ten years must show proof of successful completion of an alcohol or controlled substance treatment program in order to receive a bus driver's endorsement. For a first offense, proof of completion is required only if treatment was ordered as part of a chemical use assessment. A school district or contractor that employs a nonresident school bus driver must conduct a background check of the employee's driving record and criminal history in both Minnesota and the driver's state of residence. Convictions for disqualifying offenses, gross misdemeanors, a fourth moving violation within the previous three years, or violations of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.20">169A.20</a>, or a similar statute or ordinance in another state, must be reported to the Department of Public Safety.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.3215.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Waiver of permanent cancellation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of public safety or the commissioner's designee, in consultation with the Division of Driver and Vehicle Services, may waive the permanent cancellation requirement of this section for a person convicted of a misdemeanor, a gross misdemeanor, a nonfelony violation of chapter 152, or a felony that is not a violent crime under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.1095">609.1095</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) After notice to the requesting school district and contract provider of school bus transportation, the commissioner may waive the permanent cancellation requirement after ten years have elapsed since the person was convicted of a violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.582#stat.609.582.2">609.582, subdivision 2</a>, 3, or 4.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.3215.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.9.60.0">1991 c 265 art 9 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/603/#laws.0.18.0">1994 c 603 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.12.33.0">1994 c 647 art 12 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.2.45.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 2 s 45</a>-47; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.12.32.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 12 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/367/#laws.2.32.0">1998 c 367 art 2 s 32</a>; art 6 s 15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.9.42.0">1999 c 241 art 9 s 42</a>,43; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/478/#laws.2.7.0">2000 c 478 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/25/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 25 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/54/#laws.3.14.0">2007 c 54 art 3 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/180/#laws.0.9.0">2014 c 180 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/83/#laws.3.18.0">2017 c 83 art 3 s 18</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.3216">
        <h1 class="shn">171.3216 ACTIONS ON SCHOOL BUS ENDORSEMENT.</h1>
        <p>In addition to any authority granted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.3215">171.3215</a>, the commissioner may cancel a school bus driver's endorsement on a driver's license of any person if the commissioner determines that the person has (1) been convicted of a gross misdemeanor that the commissioner determines shows evidence that the person represents a risk to public safety, or (2) been convicted of a series of violations of law that the commissioner determines shows evidence that the person represents a risk to public safety. Upon canceling the offender's school bus driver's endorsement, the commissioner shall immediately notify the offender of the cancellation in writing, by depositing in the United States post office a notice, with postage prepaid, to the offender addressed to the offender's last known address.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.3216.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/364/#laws.0.19.0">2002 c 364 s 19</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.322">
        <h1 class="shn">171.322 AGE LIMIT FOR OPERATOR OF VEHICLE FOR HIRE.</h1>
        <p>No person under 18 years of age shall operate a motor vehicle while it is in use as a carrier of persons for hire, nor shall any person under 18 years of age drive a passenger carrying vehicle as a hired driver.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.322.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/644/#laws.0.16.0">1971 c 644 s 16</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.171.323"><b>171.323</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/142/#laws.0.4.0">1993 c 142 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.324">
        <h1 class="shn">171.324 HAZARDOUS MATERIALS ENDORSEMENT BACKGROUND CHECKS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.324.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Endorsement; fee; account; appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Before being issued or renewing a class C, class B, or class A driver's license with a hazardous materials endorsement, an applicant must comply with the federal regulations incorporated in this section.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may charge the applicant a fee of up to $100 to cover the department's actual costs of conducting the required background check of persons applying for a Minnesota driver's license with a hazardous materials endorsement. The proceeds of the fee must be deposited in an account in the special revenue fund. Money in the account is annually appropriated to the commissioner to pay the actual costs associated with conducting the required background checks.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.324.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Adoption of federal regulations.</span></h2>
          <p>Public Law 107-56, section 1012, as implemented in Code of Federal Regulations, title 49, part 1572, is incorporated by reference except for sections 1572.9 and 1572.11.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.324.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may adopt rules pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.388#stat.14.388.1">14.388, subdivision 1</a>, clause (1), in order to implement this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.324.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/295/#laws.1.14.0">2004 c 295 art 1 s 14</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.325">
        <h1 class="shn">171.325 DRIVER'S LICENSE SUSPENSIONS AND REVOCATIONS; REPORTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.325.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Issuance, suspensions, and revocations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Annually by February 15, the commissioner of public safety must report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over public safety and transportation on the status of drivers' licenses issued, suspended, and revoked. The commissioner must make the report available on the department's website.</p>
          <p>(b) At a minimum, the report must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the total number of drivers' licenses issued, suspended, and revoked as of January 1 of the year the report is submitted, broken down by county;</p>
          <p>(2) for each of the previous eight calendar years, the total number of drivers' licenses suspended and the number of suspended licenses reinstated; and</p>
          <p>(3) for each of the previous eight calendar years, the total number of drivers' licenses revoked and the number of revoked licenses reinstated.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) For purposes of paragraph (b), clauses (1), (2), and (3), the report must identify each type of suspension or revocation authorized by statute or rule and include the number of licenses suspended or revoked for each type.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.325.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Charges, convictions, and fines.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Annually by February 15, the state court administrator must report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over public safety and transportation on (1) charges and convictions for driving after suspension or revocation, and (2) payment of fines for violations related to operation of a motor vehicle. The administrator must make the report available on the state court's website.</p>
          <p>(b) At a minimum, the report must include:</p>
          <p>(1) for each of the previous eight calendar years, the number of charges under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.24">171.24, subdivisions 1</a> and 2, broken down by the charges for each subdivision and indicating whether the court appointed the public defender to represent the defendant;</p>
          <p>(2) for each of the previous eight calendar years, the number of convictions under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.24">171.24, subdivisions 1</a> and 2, broken down by the convictions for each subdivision and indicating whether the court appointed the public defender to represent the defendant; and</p>
          <p>(3) for the past calendar year, for all charges on violations related to the operation of a motor vehicle and included on the uniform fine schedule authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.101">609.101</a>, subdivision 4, the percentage of fines, broken down by whether the court appointed the public defender to represent the defendant, that:</p>
          <p>(i) were paid in full by the due date on the citation;</p>
          <p>(ii) were paid in full through a payment plan;</p>
          <p>(iii) accrued late charges;</p>
          <p>(iv) were sent to court collections; and</p>
          <p>(v) were sent to the Department of Revenue for collection.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.325.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/5/#laws.4.84.0">1Sp2021 c 5 art 4 s 84</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">DRIVER TRAINING SCHOOLS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.33">
        <h1 class="shn">171.33 DRIVER TRAINING SCHOOL; DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.33.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commercial driver training school or school.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commercial driver training school" or "school" means a business enterprise conducted by an individual, association, partnership, or corporation, which charges a fee, for the education and training of persons to drive motor vehicles or for the preparation of an applicant for a driver's license examination given by the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.33.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Instructor.</span></h2>
          <p>"Instructor" means any person, whether acting as operator of a commercial driver training school or as an employee of any such school, who teaches or supervises persons learning to drive motor vehicles or preparing to take an examination for a driver's license, and any person who supervises the work of any other instructor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.33.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of public safety.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.33.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/880/#laws.0.1.0">1967 c 880 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.1.18.0">1969 c 1129 art 1 s 18</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.335">
        <h1 class="shn">171.335 MOTORCYCLE SAFETY EDUCATION PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.335.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Established; administration; rules.</span></h2>
          <p>A motorcycle safety education program is established. The program shall be administered by the commissioner of public safety. The program shall include but is not limited to training and coordination of motorcycle safety instructors, motorcycle safety promotion and public information, and reimbursement for the cost of approved courses offered by schools and organizations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.335.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reimbursements.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of public safety, to the extent that funds are available, may reimburse schools and other approved organizations offering approved motorcycle safety education courses for up to 50 percent of the actual cost of the courses. If sufficient funds are not available, reimbursements shall be prorated. The commissioner may conduct audits and otherwise examine the records and accounts of schools and approved organizations offering the courses to insure the accuracy of the costs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.335.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) All funds in the motorcycle safety account created under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.06#stat.171.06.2a">171.06, subdivision 2a</a>, are annually appropriated to the commissioner of public safety to carry out the purposes of subdivisions 1 and 2.</p>
          <p>(b) Of the money appropriated under paragraph (a):</p>
          <p>(1) not more than five percent shall be expended to defray the administrative costs of carrying out the purposes of subdivisions 1 and 2; and</p>
          <p>(2) not more than 65 percent shall be expended for the combined purpose of training and coordinating the activities of motorcycle safety instructors and making reimbursements to schools and other approved organizations.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.335.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/583/#laws.0.3.0">1982 c 583 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/715/#laws.0.1.0">1988 c 715 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/329/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 329 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.4.51.0">1998 c 397 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/238/#laws.2.1.0">1999 c 238 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.8.1.0">2014 c 272 art 8 s 1</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/104/#laws.1.76.0">2024 c 104 art 1 s 76</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.34">
        <h1 class="shn">171.34 LICENSE FOR SCHOOL REQUIRED.</h1>
        <p>No commercial driver training school shall be established or operated after January 1, 1968, unless such school has applied for and obtained a license from the commissioner. The commissioner shall issue or adopt rules governing the requirements for a license, which may include requirements concerning location, equipment, courses of instruction, previous records of the school and instructors, financial statements, filing of schedule of maximum fees and charges, character and reputation of the instructors, insurance in such sum and with such provisions as the commissioner deems necessary to protect the interests of the public, and such other matters as the commissioner may prescribe, but the applicant shall not be required to have qualified for a teacher's certificate as required in the public school system.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.34.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/880/#laws.0.2.0">1967 c 880 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.35">
        <h1 class="shn">171.35 LICENSE FOR INSTRUCTOR REQUIRED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.35.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Licensure required.</span></h2>
          <p>No person shall act as an instructor after January 1, 1968, unless such person has applied for and obtained a license from the commissioner. The commissioner shall issue or adopt rules governing the requirements for an instructor's license, which may include requirements concerning moral character, physical condition, knowledge of the courses of instruction and of motor vehicle laws and safety principles and practices, previous personnel and employment records, teaching experience and qualifications, and such other matters as the commissioner may prescribe, but the applicant shall not be required to have qualified for a teacher's certificate as required in the public school system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.35.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Records check for instructor applicant.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Before issuing a driving instructor license, the commissioner shall conduct a criminal history and driver's license records check of the applicant. The commissioner may also conduct the check at any time while a person is so licensed. The check must consist of a criminal history check of the state criminal records repository, a national criminal history check, and a check of the driver's license records system. The commissioner shall accept the applicant's signed informed consent form for the national criminal history check request, fingerprints, and required fees. The commissioner shall submit the applicant's signed informed consent form, fingerprints, and fees to the superintendent of the Bureau of Criminal Apprehension, who is authorized to exchange the fingerprints with the Federal Bureau of Investigation to obtain the applicant's national criminal history record information. The superintendent shall also retrieve Minnesota criminal history data and provide the results of both checks to the commissioner. Using the criminal history data provided by the superintendent, the commissioner shall determine whether the applicant is disqualified from licensure. The applicant's failure to cooperate with the commissioner in conducting the records check is reasonable cause to deny an application or suspend a driving instructor license.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may issue to an otherwise qualified applicant a temporary driving instructor license, effective for no more than 180 days, upon presentation of (1) an affidavit by the applicant that the applicant has not been convicted of a gross misdemeanor or felony, and (2) a criminal history check from each state of residence. The criminal history check may be conducted and prepared by any public or private source acceptable to the commissioner. The commissioner may reissue the temporary endorsement if the national criminal records repository check is timely submitted but not completed within the 180-day period.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.35.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/880/#laws.0.3.0">1967 c 880 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/113/#laws.0.1.0">2016 c 113 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.36">
        <h1 class="shn">171.36 LICENSE FEES; RENEWAL.</h1>
        <p>All licenses expire one year from the date of issuance and may be renewed upon application to the commissioner. Each application for an original or renewal school license must be accompanied by a fee of $150 and each application for an original or renewal instructor's license must be accompanied by a fee of $50. The license fees collected under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.33">171.33</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.41">171.41</a> must be paid into the driver and vehicle services operating account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/299A.705">299A.705</a>. A license fee must not be refunded in the event that the license is rejected or revoked.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.36.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/880/#laws.0.4.0">1967 c 880 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/357/#laws.0.65.0">1981 c 357 s 65</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.43.0">1982 c 424 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/6/#laws.2.45.0">1Sp2005 c 6 art 2 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/68/#laws.7.18.0">2023 c 68 art 7 s 18</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.37">
        <h1 class="shn">171.37 INSPECTION AND EXAMINATION.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner or an authorized representative shall inspect the school facilities and equipment of applicants and licensees and shall examine applicants for instructor's licenses. The commissioner shall issue or adopt such rules and do all things necessary and proper to accomplish the purposes of this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.37.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/880/#laws.0.5.0">1967 c 880 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.38">
        <h1 class="shn">171.38 SCHOOL OR INSTRUCTOR LICENSE DENIAL OR REVOCATION.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may revoke or refuse to issue or renew a school or instructor's license in any case on finding that the applicant or licensee has not complied with, or has violated any of the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.33">171.33</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.41">171.41</a> or any rule issued or adopted pursuant thereto. Any revoked license shall be returned to the commissioner by the licensee, and its holder cannot apply for a new license for a period as determined by the commissioner not to exceed one year from the date of such revocation.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.38.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/880/#laws.0.6.0">1967 c 880 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.39">
        <h1 class="shn">171.39 EXEMPTIONS.</h1>
        <p>(a) The provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.33">171.33</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.41">171.41</a> do not apply to any person giving driver training lessons without charge; to employers maintaining driver training schools without charge for their employees only; to a home school within the meaning of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.22">120A.22</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.24">120A.24</a>; or to schools or classes conducted by colleges, universities, and high schools as a part of the normal program for those institutions.</p>
        <p>(b) Any person who is a certificated driver training instructor in a high school driver training program may give driver training instruction to persons over the age of 18 without acquiring a driver training school license or instructor's license, and those instructors may make a charge for that instruction, if there is no private commercial driver training school licensed under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.33">171.33</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.41">171.41</a> within ten miles of the municipality where driver training instruction is given and there is no adult drivers training program in effect in the schools of the school district in which the trainee resides.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.39.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/880/#laws.0.7.0">1967 c 880 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/6/#laws.0.29.0">1969 c 6 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/388/#laws.0.29.0">1998 c 388 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/238/#laws.2.28.0">1999 c 238 art 2 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/8/#laws.2.49.0">1Sp2001 c 8 art 2 s 49</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.395">
        <h1 class="shn">171.395 TELECONFERENCE DRIVER EDUCATION PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.395.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>A licensed or approved driver education program that provides both classroom and behind-the-wheel instruction may offer teleconference driver education as provided in this section. For purposes of this section, the driver education program must offer both classroom and behind-the-wheel instruction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.395.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Curriculum and instruction requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A teleconference driver education program must:</p>
          <p>(1) meet the requirements as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.0701#stat.171.0701.1">171.0701, subdivision 1</a>, and Minnesota Rules, chapter 7411, or successor rules;</p>
          <p>(2) use teleconferencing, or another similar method, that provides live synchronous distance learning and ensures that student questions and comments can be addressed in real time;</p>
          <p>(3) link all locations together with picture and sound;</p>
          <p>(4) use classroom instruction curriculum identical to the curriculum used by the driver education program in an in-person setting;</p>
          <p>(5) offer teleconference instruction to any student enrolled in the approved driver education program; and</p>
          <p>(6) provide teleconference interactive supplemental parent curriculum consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.0701#stat.171.0701.1a">171.0701, subdivision 1a</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) A student may receive teleconference instruction only if the driver education instructor verifies the student can interact with the instructor in real time with picture and sound.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.395.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/68/#laws.4.62.0">2023 c 68 art 4 s 62</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/104/#laws.1.77.0">2024 c 104 art 1 s 77</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.396">
        <h1 class="shn">171.396 ONLINE DRIVER EDUCATION PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>(a) A licensed or approved driver education program may offer online driver education as provided in this section. The online driver education program must satisfy the requirements for classroom driver education as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.0701#stat.171.0701.1">171.0701, subdivision 1</a>, and Minnesota Rules, chapter 7411. In addition, an online driver education program must:</p>
        <p>(1) include a means for the student to measure performance outcomes;</p>
        <p>(2) use a pool of rotating quiz questions;</p>
        <p>(3) incorporate accountability features to ensure the identity of the student while engaged in the course of online study;</p>
        <p>(4) measure the amount of time that the student spends in the course;</p>
        <p>(5) provide technical support to customers that is available 24 hours per day, seven days per week;</p>
        <p>(6) require a licensed Minnesota driver education instructor to monitor each student's progress and be available to answer questions in a timely manner, provided that the instructor is not required to monitor progress or answer questions in real time;</p>
        <p>(7) store course content and student data on a secure server that is protected against data breaches and is regularly backed up;</p>
        <p>(8) incorporate preventive measures in place to protect against the access of private information;</p>
        <p>(9) include the ability to update course content uniformly throughout the state; and</p>
        <p>(10) provide online interactive supplemental parental curriculum consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.0701#stat.171.0701.1a">171.0701, subdivision 1a</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) Except as required by this section, the commissioner is prohibited from imposing requirements on online driver education programs that are not equally applicable to classroom driver education programs.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.396.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/68/#laws.4.63.0">2023 c 68 art 4 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/104/#laws.1.78.0">2024 c 104 art 1 s 78</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.40">
        <h1 class="shn">171.40 DRIVER TRAINING SCHOOL VIOLATION; PETTY MISDEMEANOR.</h1>
        <p>A violation of any provision of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.33">171.33</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.41">171.41</a> or any rule issued or adopted pursuant thereto shall be a petty misdemeanor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.40.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/880/#laws.0.8.0">1967 c 880 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/6/#laws.0.30.0">1969 c 6 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/1/27/#laws.0.27.0">Ex1971 c 27 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.41">
        <h1 class="shn">171.41 COOPERATION OF STATE AGENCIES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall request and receive the assistance of other state departments and agencies in establishing rules pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.33">171.33</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.41">171.41</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.41.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/880/#laws.0.9.0">1967 c 880 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">DRIVER LICENSE COMPACT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.50">
        <h1 class="shn">171.50 DRIVER LICENSE COMPACT.</h1>
        <p>The Driver License Compact is enacted into law and entered into with all other jurisdictions legally joining in it, in the form substantially as follows:</p>
        <p class="ce">DRIVER LICENSE COMPACT</p>
        <p class="ce">ARTICLE I</p>
        <p class="ce1">Findings and Declaration of Policy</p>
        <p>(a) The party states find that:</p>
        <p>(1) The safety of their streets and highways is materially affected by the degree of compliance with state and local ordinances relating to the operation of motor vehicles.</p>
        <p>(2) Violation of such a law or ordinance is evidence that the violator engages in conduct which is likely to endanger the safety of persons and property.</p>
        <p>(3) The continuance in force of a license to drive is predicated upon compliance with laws and ordinances relating to the operation of motor vehicles, in whichever jurisdiction the vehicle is operated.</p>
        <p>(b) It is the policy of each of the party states to:</p>
        <p>(1) Promote compliance with the laws, ordinances, and administrative rules and regulations relating to the operation of motor vehicles by their operators in each of the jurisdictions where such operators drive motor vehicles.</p>
        <p>(2) Make the reciprocal recognition of licenses to drive and eligibility therefor more just and equitable by considering the overall compliance with motor vehicle laws, ordinances, and administrative rules and regulations as a condition precedent to the continuance or issuance of any license by reason of which the licensee is authorized or permitted to operate a motor vehicle in any of the party states.</p>
        <p class="ce">ARTICLE II</p>
        <p class="ce1">Definitions</p>
        <p>As used in this compact:</p>
        <p>(a) "State" means a state, territory, or possession of the United States, the District of Columbia, or the Commonwealth of Puerto Rico.</p>
        <p>(b) "Home state" means the state which has issued and has the power to suspend or revoke the use of the license or permit to operate a motor vehicle.</p>
        <p>(c) "Conviction" means a conviction of any offense related to the use or operation of a motor vehicle which is prohibited by state law, municipal ordinance, or administrative rule or regulation, or a forfeiture of bail, bond, or other security deposited to secure appearance by a person charged with having committed any such offense, and which conviction or forfeiture is required to be reported to the licensing authority.</p>
        <p class="ce">ARTICLE III</p>
        <p class="ce1">Reports of Convictions</p>
        <p>The licensing authority of a party state shall report each conviction of a person from another party state occurring within its jurisdiction to the licensing authority of the home state of the licensee. Such report shall clearly identify the person convicted; describe the violation specifying the section of the statute, code, or ordinance violated; identify the court in which action was taken; indicate whether a plea of guilty or not guilty was entered, or the conviction was a result of the forfeiture of bail, bond, or other security; and shall include any special findings made in connection therewith.</p>
        <p class="ce">ARTICLE IV</p>
        <p class="ce1">Effect of Conviction</p>
        <p>(a) The licensing authority in the home state, for the purposes of suspension, revocation, or limitation of the license to operate a motor vehicle, shall give the same effect to the conduct reported, pursuant to article III of this compact, as it would if such conduct had occurred in the home state, in the case of convictions for:</p>
        <p>(1) manslaughter or negligent homicide resulting from the operation of a motor vehicle;</p>
        <p>(2) driving a motor vehicle while under the influence of intoxicating liquor or narcotic drug, or under the influence of any other drug to a degree which renders the driver incapable of safely driving a motor vehicle;</p>
        <p>(3) any felony in the commission of which a motor vehicle is used;</p>
        <p>(4) failure to stop and render aid in the event of a motor vehicle accident resulting in the death or personal injury of another.</p>
        <p>(b) As to other convictions, reported pursuant to article III, the licensing authority in the home state shall give such effect to the conduct as provided by the laws of the home state.</p>
        <p>(c) If the laws of a party state do not provide for offenses or violations denominated or described in precisely the words employed in subdivision (a) of this article, such party state shall construe the denominations and descriptions appearing in the subdivision (a) hereof as being applicable to and identifying those offenses or violations of a substantially similar nature and the laws of such party state shall contain such provisions as may be necessary to ensure that full force and effect is given to this article.</p>
        <p class="ce">ARTICLE V</p>
        <p class="ce1">Applications for New Licenses</p>
        <p>Upon application for a license to drive, the licensing authority in a party state shall ascertain whether the applicant has ever held, or is the holder of a license to drive issued by any other party state. The licensing authority in the state where application is made shall not issue a license to drive to the applicant if:</p>
        <p>(1) The applicant has held such a license, but the same has been suspended by reason, in whole or in part, of a violation and if such suspension period has not terminated.</p>
        <p>(2) The applicant has held such a license, but the same has been revoked by reason, in whole or in part, of a violation and if such revocation has not terminated, except that after the expiration of one year from the date the license was revoked, such person may make application for a new license if permitted by law. The licensing authority may refuse to issue a license to any such applicant if, after investigation, the licensing authority determines that it will not be safe to grant to such person the privilege of driving a motor vehicle on the public highways.</p>
        <p>(3) The applicant is the holder of a license to drive issued by another party state and currently in force unless the applicant surrenders such license.</p>
        <p class="ce">ARTICLE VI</p>
        <p class="ce1">Applicability of Other Laws</p>
        <p>Except as expressly required by provisions of this compact, nothing contained herein shall be construed to affect the right of any party state to apply any of its other laws relating to licenses to drive to any person or circumstance, nor to invalidate or prevent any driver license agreement or other cooperative arrangement between a party state and a nonparty state.</p>
        <p class="ce">ARTICLE VII</p>
        <p class="ce1">Compact Administrator and Interchange of Information</p>
        <p>(a) The head of the licensing authority of each party state shall be the administrator of this compact for the state. The administrators, acting jointly, shall have the power to formulate all necessary and proper procedures for the exchange of information under this compact.</p>
        <p>(b) The administrator of each party state shall furnish to the administrator of each other party state any information or documents reasonably necessary to facilitate the administration of this compact.</p>
        <p class="ce">ARTICLE VIII</p>
        <p class="ce1">Entry Into Force and Withdrawal</p>
        <p>(a) This compact shall enter into force and become effective as to any state when it has enacted the same into law.</p>
        <p>(b) Any party state may withdraw from this compact by enacting a statute repealing the same, but no such withdrawal shall take effect until six months after the executive head of the withdrawing state has given notice of the withdrawal to the executive heads of all other party states. No withdrawal shall affect the validity or applicability by the licensing authorities of states remaining party to the compact of any report of conviction occurring prior to the withdrawal.</p>
        <p class="ce">ARTICLE IX</p>
        <p class="ce1">Construction and Severability</p>
        <p>This compact shall be liberally construed so as to effectuate the purposes thereof. The provisions of this compact shall be severable and if any phrase, clause, sentence, or provision of this compact is declared to be contrary to the constitution of any party state or of the United States, or the applicability thereof to any government, agency, person, or circumstance is held invalid, the validity of the remainder of this compact and the applicability thereof to any government, agency, person, or circumstance shall not be affected thereby. If this compact shall be held contrary to the constitution of any state party thereto, the compact shall remain in full force and effect as to the remaining states and in full force and effect as to the state affected as to all severable matters.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.50.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/307/#laws.0.35.0">1989 c 307 s 35</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.51">
        <h1 class="shn">171.51 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.51.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Executive head.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.50">171.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.56">171.56</a>, with reference to this state, the term "executive head" means the governor of Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.51.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Licensing authority.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.50">171.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.56">171.56</a>, the term "licensing authority" with reference to this state means the Driver's License Division of the Minnesota Department of Public Safety.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.51.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/307/#laws.0.36.0">1989 c 307 s 36</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.52">
        <h1 class="shn">171.52 TRANSFER OF INFORMATION.</h1>
        <p>The licensing authority shall furnish to the appropriate authorities of any other party state information or documents reasonably necessary to facilitate the administration of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.50">171.50</a>, articles III, IV, and V.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.52.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/307/#laws.0.37.0">1989 c 307 s 37</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.53">
        <h1 class="shn">171.53 COMPACT ADMINISTRATOR.</h1>
        <p>The compact administrator provided for in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.50">171.50</a>, article VII, is not entitled to additional compensation on account of service as administrator, but is entitled to expenses incurred in connection with the duties and responsibilities as administrator, in the same manner as for expenses incurred in connection with other duties or responsibilities of employment. The compact administrator shall represent this state or shall appoint a representative for this state in the Driver License Compact Commission, and that commission has the authority to formulate procedures for exchanging information, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.50">171.50</a>, article VII.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.53.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/307/#laws.0.38.0">1989 c 307 s 38</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.54">
        <h1 class="shn">171.54 REPORTING TO LICENSING AUTHORITY.</h1>
        <p>A court or other agency or political subdivision of this state, that has jurisdiction to take action regarding the suspension, revocation, cancellation, or other limitation of a license to drive, shall promptly report the action and the circumstances upon which it is based to the licensing authority on forms furnished by the department.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.54.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/307/#laws.0.39.0">1989 c 307 s 39</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.55">
        <h1 class="shn">171.55 OUT-OF-STATE CONVICTIONS GIVEN EFFECT.</h1>
        <p class="in">The commissioner shall give the same effect for driver licensing purposes to conduct reported from a licensing authority or court in another state or province or territory of Canada that the commissioner would give to conduct reported from a court or other agency of this state, whether or not the other state or province or territory of Canada is a party to the Driver License Compact in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.50">171.50</a>. The conduct to be given effect by the commissioner includes a report of conviction for an offense enumerated in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.50">171.50</a>, article IV, or an offense described in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.17">171.17</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/171.18">171.18</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.55.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/307/#laws.0.40.0">1989 c 307 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/54/#laws.3.6.0">2007 c 54 art 3 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.56">
        <h1 class="shn">171.56 FILING OF BYLAWS AND AMENDMENTS.</h1>
        <p>The Driver License Compact Commission shall file a copy of its bylaws and amendments to the bylaws with the Minnesota secretary of state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.56.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/307/#laws.0.41.0">1989 c 307 s 41</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">MISCELLANEOUS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.171.60">
        <h1 class="shn">171.60 MOTORCYCLE ROAD GUARD CERTIFICATE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.60.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Certificate required.</span></h2>
          <p>No person may perform traffic control as a motorcycle road guard as provided under chapter 169 without a valid motorcycle road guard certificate issued by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.60.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Certification qualifications and standards.</span></h2>
          <p>Through the Minnesota Motorcycle Safety Center, the commissioner of public safety shall:</p>
          <p>(1) establish qualifications and requirements for a person to obtain a motorcycle road guard certificate under this section, which must include:</p>
          <p>(i) a minimum 18 years of age;</p>
          <p>(ii) possession of a valid driver's license; and</p>
          <p>(iii) successful completion of a motorcycle road guard certification course;</p>
          <p>(2) develop and offer, whether by the Minnesota Motorcycle Safety Center or authorized agents, a motorcycle road guard certification course; and</p>
          <p>(3) establish safety and equipment standards for a person who operates under a motorcycle road guard certificate, including but not limited to specifying requirements for a reflective safety vest.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.60.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Fee.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of public safety shall assess a fee for each applicant for a motorcycle road guard certificate, calculated to cover the commissioner's cost of establishing and administering the program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.60.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who violates any provision of this section is guilty of a petty misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.171.60.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Rulemaking.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of public safety shall adopt rules to carry out the provisions of this section. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1283">16A.1283</a>, the rules must specify the fee to be assessed under subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.171.60.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/287/#laws.3.43.0">2012 c 287 art 3 s 43</a>
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
